B routine priorityPublic-health news

Measles case confirmed in western Kentucky, health officials suspect outbreak - Louisville Public Media

News Brief
A measles case has been confirmed in western Kentucky, with health officials suspecting an outbreak. The report, published by Louisville Public Media on August 14, 2026, highlights the presence of the measles virus in the region. While the confirmation of a single case is noted, no further details on the number of cases, deaths, or specific containment measures are provided. The report indicates that health officials are monitoring the situation closely, but the evidence does not specify the extent of the outbreak or the risk level. This information is crucial for public health awareness and response planning in the area.
Original source text (English)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

埃迪县首例 麻疹 病例报告于 Artesia - Carlsbad Current Argus

Eddy County’s first case of measles was reported in Artesia - Carlsbad Current Argus
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 14 日,新墨西哥州卫生部报告了埃迪县(特别是阿蒂西亚)首例 麻疹 病例。该患者“很可能”是在州外感染的,但在抵达新墨西哥州后出现症状,并在埃迪县报告了这些症状。该报告没有提供有关病例、死亡或接触人数的更多详细信息,也没有提及检测、隔离或接触者追踪等具体应对措施。该案件已得到证实,但消息人士没有详细说明更广泛的影响或风险评估。此信息是新闻文章的一部分,其中还包括与 麻疹 案件无关的不相关主题,例如土地租赁、分区变更和社区活动。该报告强调了监测和应对此类健康事件的重要性,特别是在有可能发生疾病传播的地区。
查看新闻五要素
时间
根据新墨西哥州卫生部 (N1) 的新闻稿,首例 麻疹 病例于 2026 年 8 月 14 日报告。
地点与对象
该病例在埃迪县报告,特别是在新墨西哥州阿蒂西亚 (N1)。
事件
埃迪县首例 麻疹 病例报告于阿蒂西亚,该患者很可能在州外感染,但在抵达新墨西哥州 (N1) 后出现症状。
规模、影响与风险
该报告仅提到了 1 例 麻疹 确诊病例,没有提供有关病例、死亡或暴露的更多详细信息 (N1)。
应对、状态与不确定性
新墨西哥州卫生部报告了该病例,但没有提及有关调查、检测或应对措施的进一步细节 (N1)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Eddy County’s first case of measles was reported in Artesia - Carlsbad Current Argus

News Brief
On August 14, 2026, the New Mexico Department of Health reported the first case of measles in Eddy County, specifically in Artesia. The patient was 'most likely' infected out of state but developed symptoms after arriving in New Mexico and reported them while in Eddy County. The report does not provide additional details on the number of cases, deaths, or exposures, nor does it mention specific response measures such as testing, isolation, or contact tracing. The case is confirmed, but the source does not elaborate on the broader implications or risk assessment. This information is part of a news article that also includes unrelated topics such as land leasing, zoning changes, and community events, which are not relevant to the measles case. The report highlights the importance of monitoring and addressing such health events, especially in areas with potential for disease spread.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

克里斯蒂安县确诊 麻疹 病例;暴发 疑似 - Hoptown Chronicle

Case of measles confirmed in Christian County; outbreak suspected - Hoptown Chronicle
新闻简报2026 年 8 月 13 日,肯塔基州克里斯蒂安县确诊一例 麻疹 病例,促使当地和州卫生官员怀疑 麻疹 病例,并敦促立即进行社区免疫。当局强调需要使用 疫苗接种 来防止进一步传播,尽管现有证据中没有报告更多病例、死亡或暴露计数。对此的反应仍在积极调查中,卫生官员正在监测进一步的事态发展。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-14;据报道,该事件发生于 2026 年 8 月 13 日。
地点与对象

Case of measles confirmed in Christian County; outbreak suspected - Hoptown Chronicle

News Brief
On August 13, 2026, a single measles case was confirmed in Christian County, Kentucky, prompting local and state health officials to suspect an outbreak and urge immediate community immunization. Authorities emphasized the need for vaccination to prevent further spread, though no additional cases, deaths, or exposure counts were reported in the available evidence. The response remains under active investigation, with health officials monitoring for further developments.

新麻疹在Artesia曝光 - KUNM

New Measles exposure in Artesia - KUNM
新闻简报新墨西哥州卫生部于 8 月 13 日确认了该州 2026 年第 18 例 麻疹 病例,报告称,一名人员可能于 8 月 6 日至 11 日期间在埃迪县阿蒂西亚接触了其他人。接触发生在联邦执法培训中心、沃尔玛商店和阿蒂西亚总医院急诊室。卫生官员怀疑感染是在州外传播的,并敦促当时在场的任何人检查 疫苗接种 的状态,并在接下来的三周内观察是否出现发烧、咳嗽、皮疹等症状。州公共卫生办公室可为未参保或参保不足的居民提供免费 MMR 疫苗。目前还没有与此次暴露相关的死亡报告。
查看新闻五要素
时间
Published 2026-08-13 at 4:32 PM MDT; exposure period 2026-08-06 through 2026-08-11.
地点与对象
阿蒂西亚,艾迪县,新墨西哥州,美国;受影响的地点包括联邦执法培训中心、沃尔玛和阿蒂西亚综合医院急诊室。
事件
新墨西哥州卫生部确认了该州 2026 年第 18 例 麻疹 病例,该病例于 2026 年 8 月 6 日至 11 日在阿蒂西亚公开暴露。该病例已确诊,并可能是在州外感染的。
规模、影响与风险
2026 年新墨西哥州共有 18 起 麻疹 病例;此次暴露事件中没有死亡报告。在指定时间和地点暴露的未接种疫苗的个体中,仍然可能存在继发性 传播 的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
新墨西哥州卫生部建议接触者核实 麻疹 状态并监测 麻疹 症状(接触后 7-21 天)。公共卫生办公室提供免费或优惠的 MMR 疫苗接种。目前状态:正在调查并积极进行公共卫生外展。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

New Measles exposure in Artesia - KUNM

News Brief
The New Mexico Department of Health confirmed the state’s 18th measles case of 2026 on August 13, reporting that an individual likely exposed others in Artesia, Eddy County, from August 6 to 11. The exposure occurred at the Federal Law Enforcement Training Center, a Walmart store, and Artesia General Hospital’s emergency room. Health officials suspect the infection was acquired out-of-state and urge anyone present during those times to check vaccination status and watch for symptoms—fever, cough, rash—over the next three weeks. Free MMR vaccines are available at state public health offices for uninsured or underinsured residents. No deaths have been reported in connection with this exposure.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content 89.9 FM Live From The University Of New Mexico Public Health New Mexico New measles exposure in Artesia KUNM | By Daniel Montaño Published August 13, 2026 at 4:32 PM MDT Facebook Email Print Your browser doesn’t support HTML5 audio The measles, Mumps and Rubella vaccine is this best protection against measles according to state officials. An individual with measles, the 18th case in New Mexico so far this year, might have exposed members of the public to the virus from August 6 through August 11 in Artesia. Annie Rice The New Mexico Department of Health announced Thursday a new measles case has been confirmed in Eddy County in the Southeast, bringing the state's case count to 18 total in 2026. People may have been exposed to this new case at locations around Artesia during the second week of August. This newest case of measles was most likely acquired out-of-state, according to the DOH, with the individual not displaying symptoms until after arriving in New Mexico. Anyone within Artesia could have been exposed at the following locations and times: From August 6 through August 10 at the Federal Law Enforcement Training Center at 1793 N. 13th Street On the morning of August 9 at the Walmart, 604 N. 26th Street From August 10 at 9:15 p.m. through August 11 3:50 a.m. at the Artesia General Hospital Emergency room, 702 N. 13th Street Anyone who was at those locations during those times should check their vaccination status and report any measles symptoms within the next three weeks to a health care provider. Measles symptoms develop between seven to 21 days after exposure, beginning with cold or flu-like symptoms — fever, runny nose, cough and red eyes. A red spotted rash usually then appears starting on the head or face and spreading down from there. Although New Mexico did experience a Measles outbreak in 2025, this is the first case of measles in the land of Enchantment since the end of May. New Mexico Deputy State Epidemiologist Dr. Chad Smelser said vaccination is still the best tool to prevent measles. NMDOH offers free or reduced cost vaccinations at its public health offices across the state for children and adults who are uninsured or underinsured. For more information, call the NMDOH Helpline at 1-833-SWNURSE (1-833-796-8773) or visit their measles guidance website. Support for this coverage comes from the W.K. Kellogg Foundation. Public Health New MexicomeaslesNMDOHNew Mexico Department of HealthDr. Chad SmelserEddy County Facebook Email Print Daniel Montaño Daniel Montaño is a reporter with KUNM's Public Health, Poverty and Equity project. He is also an occasional host of Morning Edition, All Things Considered, and Let's Talk New Mexico since 2021, is a born and bred Burqueño who first started with KUNM about two decades ago, as a production assistant while he was in high school. During the intervening years, he studied journalism at UNM, lived abroad, fell in and out of love, conquered here and there, failed here and there, and developed a taste for advocating for human rights. Response, status and uncertainty

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

卫生部表示,随着宾夕法尼亚州 麻疹 病例的增加,免疫率也在增加

As Pennsylvania measles cases grow, so do immunization rates, says DOH
新闻简报据宾夕法尼亚州卫生部称,自 2026 年 4 月以来,宾夕法尼亚州的 麻疹 确诊病例已增至 258 例,过去一周报告了 66 例,自 6 月中旬以来约克县报告了 14 例。兰开斯特县仍然是疫情中心,有 145 例病例,而 18 至 49 岁的成年人占感染的大多数。尽管疫苗接种率激增,但官员们强调免疫接种率不断上升,包括自 6 月以来接种了 1,949 例 麻疹 疫苗接种,幼儿园 疫苗接种 接种率超过 93%,这支持了强大的社区免疫力。卫生部强调,两剂 MMR 疫苗 提供 97% 的终生保护,并通过药房合作伙伴扩大了使用范围。与此同时,宾夕法尼亚州州长批评了最近的一项联邦行政命令,该命令减少了推荐儿童 疫苗 的数量,重申了该州对基于科学的公共卫生指导的承诺。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-08-13;该事件自 2026 年 6 月中旬以来一直在持续,据报道,最近 7 天出现了增长。
地点与对象
美国宾夕法尼亚州;约克县和全州 19 个县;18-49 岁的成年人受影响最严重;疫苗接种 覆盖率较高的学龄儿童。
事件
随着病例的增加,宾夕法尼亚州卫生部 (DOH) 报告了 麻疹 暴发;自 4 月以来,卫生部已确认全州范围内有 258 例病例,其中过去 7 天内有 66 例,约克县自 6 月中旬以来有 14 例;卫生部发言人表示,由于 疫苗接种 比率较高,学生面临的风险较低。
规模、影响与风险
自2026年4月以来,宾夕法尼亚州确诊了258例麻疹病例,其中4月份以来有246例;过去 7 天内报告了 66 例病例;自6月中旬以来,约克县已有14例病例;19个县受到影响;自4月份以来,18-49岁的成年人有151例;0-17岁儿童占81例;兰卡斯特县震中有 145 例(自 4 月以来有 137 例);切斯特县自 4 月份以来已有 24 例病例;卫生部注意到幼儿园 疫苗接种 率较高(>93%)和强大的社区免疫力。
应对、状态与不确定性

B routine priorityPublic-health news

As Pennsylvania measles cases grow, so do immunization rates, says DOH

News Brief
Pennsylvania’s measles outbreak has grown to 258 confirmed cases since April 2026, with 66 reported in the past week and 14 in York County since mid-June, according to the state Department of Health. Lancaster County remains the epicenter with 145 cases, while adults aged 18–49 account for the majority of infections. Despite the surge, officials highlight rising immunization rates, including 1,949 measles vaccinations administered since June and a high kindergarten vaccination rate exceeding 93%, which supports robust community immunity. The Department of Health stresses that two doses of the MMR vaccine provide 97% lifetime protection and has expanded access through pharmacy partnerships. Meanwhile, Pennsylvania’s governor criticized a recent federal executive order that reduces the number of recommended childhood vaccines, reaffirming the state’s commitment to science-based public health guidance.
Original source text (English)
Measles cases continue to grow in York and surrounding counties as many school districts around the commonwealth are beginning to start a new year. According to numbers released by the Pennsylvania Department of Health (DOH) Wednesday, Aug. 12, York County has reported 14 cases of measles to the DOH since mid-June. There have been 66 cases reported around the state in the last seven days. Of the 258 cases reported around the state, 246 have come since April. More: Want a new pet? Free adoption events set at York County SPCA More: Rutter's eyes alcohol sales at southern York County convenience store The number of counties reporting at least one measles case has grown to 19. There were only three counties reporting measles cases for the first three months of 2026. Lancaster County continues to be the epicenter of the disease in Pennsylvania with 145. Of those, 137 have come since April. Nearby Chester County has had 24 cases reported since April. Adults between the ages of 18 and 49 have had the most reported cases of the disease. According to the DOH, 151 cases since April have been reported in that age group while cases among children 0 to 17 number 81 in the same period. While measles cases have grown, so have immunization numbers have grown in the last three months. Of the more than 2,700 immunizations administered by the DOH this year, 1,949 measles immunizations have been administered since June. >> Want to stay up to date on the latest news? Sign up for our newsletters. DOH spokesperson Neil Ruhland said that the best protection against getting the measles is getting immunized, and the majority of Pennsylvanians seem to be getting the message. “School-aged children in Pennsylvania have a high vaccination rate, with more than 93% of all kindergarteners being fully vaccinated against measles,” Ruhland said. “Since the majority of students are fully vaccinated, our schools benefit from robust community immunity that serves as a powerful barrier against the virus, so the risk of measles is low for Pennsylvania students as schools begin their fall classes.” Please consider subscribing to support local journalism. He said since the majority of students are fully vaccinated, Pennsylvania schools benefit from robust community immunity that serves as a powerful barrier against the virus. “So, the risk of measles is low for Pennsylvania students as schools begin their fall classes,” Ruhland said. Ruhland said, after two doses of the MMR vaccine, it provides 97% lifetime protection from contracting measles. Pharmacies inside Giant Food Stores are now offering the MMR vaccine. Go HERE to make an appointment at a store near you. The DOH launched a new dashboard Aug. 3 to show parents the immunization rates among kindergarten, seventh grade and 12th grade students on individual school campuses. The dashboard can be found at School Immunization Rates . Executive order decreases recommended immunizations Just as immunization numbers are beginning to grow in Pennsylvania ahead of school starting, President Donald Trump signed an executive order to change immunization rules. The executive order signed by the President Monday, Aug. 10 recommends all children get immunizations to provide protection for 11 diseases – a decrease from the 18 diseases recommended by the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention in 2024. Immunizations no longer recommended for all children are now recommended for shared clinical decision-making, ensuring that parents have all options available to them. The executive order also recommends separating out the combined MMR vaccine, which is used to prevent the measles, into three single-disease shots and recommends administering all vaccines during separate medical visits to give parents more options regarding the timing and frequency of this vaccine administration. Gov. Josh Shapiro said on social media Monday in response to the executive order that Pennsylvania will continue to give public health guidance based on science and facts. “Once again, Donald Trump and (Department of Health and Human Services Secretary) RFK Jr. are screwing around with the childhood vaccine schedule — ignoring decades of science, sowing chaos and confusion, and spreading dangerous misinformation that put kids and families at risk,” Shapiro said. The state, Shapiro said, has already taken RFK Jr. to court to protect Pennsylvanians' freedom to make the best health care decisions for themselves and their kids. “Because here in Pennsylvania, we trust experts, doctors, and parents to make the best decisions for our families — not conspiracy theorists,” Shapiro said. York Daily Record reporter Meredith Willse contributed to this report. This article originally appeared on York Daily Record: As Pennsylvania measles cases grow, so do immunization rates, says DOH
